## Exhibit 99.1

EXHIBIT 99.1

Notice to LSE

**Total Voting Rights and Issued Capital**

1 October 2026

In accordance with the Financial Conduct Authority’s (‘FCA’) Disclosure Guidance and Transparency Rule 5.6.1R, Rio Tinto plc notifies the market that as of 30 September 2026:

1. Rio Tinto plc’s issued share capital comprised 1,256,051,053 ordinary shares of 10p each, each with one vote.

2. 867,389 ordinary shares of 10p each are held in treasury. These shares are not taken into consideration in relation to the payment of dividends and voting at shareholder meetings.

Accordingly, the total number of voting rights in Rio Tinto plc is 1,255,183,664. This figure may be used by shareholders (and others with notification obligations) as the denominator for the calculation by which they will determine if they are required to notify their interest in, or a change to their interest in, Rio Tinto plc under the FCA’s Disclosure Guidance and Transparency Rules.

Note :

As at the date of this announcement:

(a) Rio Tinto plc has also issued one Special Voting Share of 10p and one DLC Dividend Share of 10p in connection with its dual listed companies (‘DLC’) merger with Rio Tinto Limited which was designed to place the shareholders of both companies in substantially the same position as if they held shares in a single enterprise owning all of the assets of both companies;

(b) the Special Voting Share facilitates joint voting by shareholders of Rio Tinto plc and Rio Tinto Limited on joint electorate resolutions; and

(c) there are 371,821,214 publicly held Rio Tinto Limited shares in issue which do not form part of the share capital of Rio Tinto plc.

LEI: 213800YOEO5OQ72G2R82

## Exhibit 99.2

EXHIBIT 99.2

Notice to LSE

**Issue of Shares**

1 October 2026

In accordance with PRM 1.6.4R, Rio Tinto plc (the "Company") (LEI:213800YOEO5OQ72G2R82) confirms that between 1 September 2026 and 30 September 2026, it has issued and allotted 140 ordinary shares of 10 pence each (ISIN GB0007188757) ("Shares") to satisfy awards under the Rio Tinto plc Global Employee Share Plan.

The Shares were admitted to trading on the London Stock Exchange's Main Market under the Company’s existing block admission arrangements and rank equally with the Company’s existing ordinary shares.

Following this issuance of Shares, the Company confirms that as at 30 September 2026, the Company's issued share capital comprised 1,256,051,053 Shares, each with one vote. 867,389 Shares are held in treasury. Accordingly, the total number of voting rights in the Company is 1,255,183,664.

## Exhibit 99.3

EXHIBIT 99.3

Media Release

**Rio Tinto, Tasmanian Government and Commonwealth Government secure the future of Bell Bay Aluminium until 2031**

01 October 2026

MELBOURNE, Australia--(BUSINESS WIRE)-- Rio Tinto, the Tasmanian Government and the Australian Government have reached agreement to secure the ongoing operation of Bell Bay Aluminium in northern Tasmania through to the end of 2031.

Under the arrangements, Hydro Tasmania will continue supplying electricity to Bell Bay Aluminium until 31 December 2031, and the Australian and Tasmanian Governments will provide additional support to continue operations as Tasmania’s energy system evolves.

The additional support will help maintain Bell Bay Aluminium’s international competitiveness and its ongoing contribution to the Tasmanian economy.

Rio Tinto Aluminium & Lithium Chief Executive Jérôme Pécresse said: “These agreements will provide Bell Bay Aluminium with an operating pathway through to 2031, and increased certainty for our people, suppliers and the northern Tasmanian community.

“Bell Bay Aluminium has been part of Tasmania's heritage for 71 years, and we are proud of the jobs, exports and economic value it creates for the State.

“With these agreements in place, we can continue to safely deliver high-quality aluminium products for our customers in Australia and around the world.

“I would like to thank the Tasmanian and Australian Governments, Hydro Tasmania, our workers and everyone else who contributed to securing this outcome.”

About Bell Bay Aluminium

Bell Bay Aluminium is 100% owned by Rio Tinto and began operating in 1955. It was the first aluminium smelter in the Southern Hemisphere and produces about 190,000 tonnes of aluminium a year.

The smelter supports about 550 full-time employees and indirectly supports more than 1200 jobs. It spends about A\$260 million annually with 180 suppliers.

Rio Tinto's integrated Australian aluminium production chain of bauxite mines, alumina refineries and aluminium smelters is a significant economic driver, employing over 5000 people directly and supporting thousands more Australian livelihoods.

Bell Bay’s current power supply arrangement with Hydro Tasmania ends on 31 December 2026.

## Exhibit 99.4

EXHIBIT 99.4

Notice to ASX/LSE

**Shareholdings of persons discharging managerial responsibility (PDMR) / Key Management Personnel (KMP)**

2 October 2026

As part of its dual listed company structure, Rio Tinto notifies dealings in Rio Tinto plc and Rio Tinto Limited securities by PDMRs / KMPs to both the Australian Securities Exchange (ASX) and the London Stock Exchange (LSE).

**Share Plan Account (SPA)**

Rio Tinto plc ordinary shares of 10p each and Rio Tinto Limited ordinary shares held in a SPA are eligible for a dividend reinvestment whereby cash dividends are used to buy Rio Tinto shares in the market.

On 29 September 2026 the following PDMR/KMP(s) acquired shares in Rio Tinto Limited by way of reinvestment of dividends received on shares held in the SPA.

If the relevant interests of a group of persons are essentially similar, they may be referred to throughout the form as a specifically named group if the membership of each group, with the names and addresses of members is clearly set out in paragraph 7 of the form. (2) S ee the definition of "associate" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (3) S ee the definition of "relevant interest" in sections 608 and 671B(7) of the Corporations Act 2001. (4) T he voting shares of a company constitute one class unless divided into separate classes.

603 Page 14 of 14 (5) The total number of votes attached to all the voting shares in the company or voting interests in the scheme (if any) that the person or an associate has a relevant interest in. (6) T he person's votes divided by the total votes in the body corporate or scheme multiplied by 100. (7) Include details of: (a) any relevant agreement or other circumstances by which the relevant interest was acquired. If subsection 671B(4) applies, a copy of any document setting out the terms of any relevant agreement, and a statement by the person giving full and accurate details of any contract, scheme or arrangement, must accompany this form, together with a written statement certifying this contract, scheme or arrangement; and (b) any qualification of the power of a person to exercise, control the exercise of, or influence the exercise of, the voting powers or disposal of the securities to which the relevant interest relates (indicating clearly the particular securities to which the qualification applies). See the definition of "relevant agreement" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (8) If the substantial holder is unable to determine the identity of the person ( eg. if the relevant interest arises because of an option) write "unknown". (9) Details of the consideration must include any and all benefits, money and other, that any person from whom a relevant interest was acquired has, or may, become entitled to receive in relation to that acquisition. Details must be included even if the benefit is conditional on the happening or not of a contingency. Details must be included of any benefit paid on behalf of the substantial holder or its associate in relation to the acquisitions, even if they are not paid directly to the person from whom the relevant interest was acquired.

Date: 06-Oct-2026 Company’s name: RIO TINTO LTD ISIN: AU000000RIO1 Date of change of relevant interests: 02-Oct-2026 Schedule Type of agreement Master Securities Lending Agreement (“MSLA”) Parties to agreement JPMorgan Chase Bank, N.A. (acting as agent) (“lender”) and BMO CAPITAL MARKETS CORP ( “Borrower”) Transfer date Settlement Date: 02-Oct-2026 Holder of voting rights Borrower Are there any restriction on voting rights Yes If yes, detail The borrower undertakes to use its best endeavors to arrange for the voting rights to be exercised in accordance with the instructions of the lender, provided that the lender uses its best endeavors to notify the borrower of its instructions in writing no later than 7 business days prior to the date upon which such votes are exercisable or as otherwise agreed between the parties. This undertaking is set out as per the rights of the borrower as stated in the MSLA. Scheduled return date (if any) None Does the borrower have the right to return early? Yes If yes, detail Borrower has right to return all and any equivalent securities early at any time in accordance with the lender’s instructions. Does the lender have the right to recall early? Yes If yes, detail Lender has right to recall all or any equivalent securities at any time by giving notice on any business day of not less than the standard settlement time for such equivalent securities on the exchange or in the clearing organisation through which the relevant borrowed securities were originally delivered (and where there is a difference between the settlement time for sales and purchases on the relevant exchange or clearing organisation, the standard settlement time shall be the shorter of the two times). The borrower must return the securities not later than the expiry of such notice in accordance with the lender’s instructions. Will the securities be returned on settlement? Yes If yes, detail any exceptions No exceptions Statement If requested by the company to whom the prescribed form must be given, or if requested by ASIC, a copy of the agreement will be given to that company or ASIC.

605 Page 3 of 3 DIRECTIONS (1) If there are a number of substantial holders with similar or related relevant interests (e.g. a corporation and its related corporations, or the manager and trustee of an equity trust), the names could be included in an annexure to the form. If the relevant interests of a group of persons are essentially similar, they may be referred to throughout the form as a specifically named group if the membership of each group, with the names and addresses of members is clearly set out in paragraph 4 of the form. (2) See the definition of "relevant interest" in sections 608 and 671B(7) of the Corporations Act 2001. (3) See the definition of "associate" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (4) Include details of: (a) any relevant agreement or other circumstances because of which the change in relevant interest occurred. If subsection 671B(4) applies, a copy of any document setting out the terms of any relevant agreement, and a statement by the person giving full and accurate details of any contract, scheme or arrangement, must accompany this form, together with a written statement certifying this contract, scheme or arrangement; and (b) any qualification of the power of a person to exercise, control the exercise of, or influence the exercise of, the voting powers or disposal of the securities to which the relevant interest relates (indicating clearly the particular securities to which the qualification applies). See the definition of "relevant agreement" in section 9 of the Corporations Act 2001. (5) Details of the consideration must include any and all benefits, money and other, that any person from whom a relevant interest was acquired has, or may, become entitled to receive in relation to that acquisition. Details must be included even if the benefit is conditional n the happening or not of a contingency. Details must be included of any benefit paid on behalf of the substantial holder or its associate in relation to the acquisitions, even if they are not paid directly to the person from whom the relevant interest was acquired. (6) T he voting shares of a company constitute one class unless divided into separate classes. (7) Give details, if appropriate, of the present association and any change in that association since the last substantial holding notice.

Appendix: Prescribed information pursuant to securities lending arrangement disclosed under the substantial shareholding notice filed with ASX. Date: 07-Oct-2026 Company’s name: RIO TINTO LTD ISIN: AU000000RIO1 Date of change of relevant interests: 05-Oct-2026 Schedule Type of agreement Master Securities Lending Agreement (“MSLA”) Parties to agreement JPMorgan Chase Bank, N.A. (acting as agent) (“lender”) and BMO CAPITAL MARKETS CORP ( “Borrower”) Transfer date Settlement Date: 02-Oct-2026 Holder of voting rights Borrower Are there any restriction on voting rights Yes If yes, detail The borrower undertakes to use its best endeavors to arrange for the voting rights to be exercised in accordance with the instructions of the lender, provided that the lender uses its best endeavors to notify the borrower of its instructions in writing no later than 7 business days prior to the date upon which such votes are exercisable or as otherwise agreed between the parties. This undertaking is set out as per the rights of the borrower as stated in the MSLA. Scheduled return date (if any) None Does the borrower have the right to return early? Yes If yes, detail Borrower has right to return all and any equivalent securities early at any time in accordance with the lender’s instructions. Does the lender have the right to recall early? Yes If yes, detail Lender has right to recall all or any equivalent securities at any time by giving notice on any business day of not less than the standard settlement time for such equivalent securities on the exchange or in the clearing organisation through which the relevant borrowed securities were originally delivered (and where there is a difference between the settlement time for sales and purchases on the relevant exchange or clearing organisation, the standard settlement time shall be the shorter of the two times). The borrower must return the securities not later than the expiry of such notice in accordance with the lender’s instructions. Will the securities be returned on settlement? Yes If yes, detail any exceptions No exceptions Statement If requested by the company to whom the prescribed form must be given, or if requested by ASIC, a copy of the agreement will be given to that company or ASIC.

Rio Tinto plc Dividend Reinvestment Plan Rio Tinto plc offers a dividend reinvestment plan that gives shareholders the opportunity to use their cash dividends to buy Rio Tinto plc shares in the market. Notice to ASX/LSE

**Shareholdings of persons discharging managerial responsibility (PDMR) / Key Management Personnel (KMP)**

22 October 2026

As part of its dual listed company structure, Rio Tinto notifies dealings in Rio Tinto plc and Rio Tinto Limited securities by PDMRs / KMPs to both the Australian Securities Exchange (ASX) and the London Stock Exchange (LSE).

**Global Employee Share Plan (myShare)**

Under myShare employees are able to purchase, on a quarterly basis, Rio Tinto plc ordinary shares of 10p each, Rio Tinto plc ADRs or Rio Tinto Limited ordinary shares. Shares are purchased out of monthly deductions from salary and participants are allocated an equivalent number of shares or ADRs free of charge (‘matching shares’ and ‘matching ADRs’), conditional upon satisfying the terms of myShare. The matching shares or matching ADRs are subject to a three year holding period, and vest after this period.

**myShare vesting**

## Exhibit 99.12

Notice to ASX/LSE Rio Tinto to invest \$1.9 billion to develop the Kangwinan bauxite project in Western Cape York, Queensland 23 October 2026 MELBOURNE, Australia-- Rio Tinto will invest \$1.9 billion1 to develop the Kangwinan bauxite project on Western Cape York Peninsula in Queensland, underpinning the long-term future of its Weipa Operations and continued supply to its international customers and its Australian alumina refineries. Kangwinan highlights Rio Tinto’s continued commitment to mining in Australia and will sustain its position as one of the world’s leading high-quality bauxite producers. This brownfield investment will leverage Rio Tinto’s project execution capability and reflects continued application of the company’s disciplined capital allocation framework. It will add 20 million tonnes of annual bauxite production capacity in Weipa, leveraging some existing infrastructure, with first output targeted in 2029 and full production expected by 2032. The project will replace production from the Andoom mine near Weipa as it reaches the end of its life this decade, while increasing Weipa Operations’ annual capacity to about 44 million tonnes, from current production of around 38 million tonnes per year. It will also help compensate for the planned end of production at the Gove mine in the Northern Territory in 2029. Rio Tinto Aluminium & Lithium Chief Executive Jérôme Pécresse said: “Kangwinan is a strong vote of confidence in Weipa, in the quality of its bauxite, the capability of its workforce, and the future of the communities and businesses closely connected to our operations. “The project represents the most significant new mining investment in Queensland in recent years and is a sign of Rio Tinto’s long-term commitment to its bauxite business, the State and our integrated mine-to-metal aluminium value chain. “For more than 60 years, we have operated in Western Cape York, building a world-class bauxite business and enduring relationships with Traditional Owners and local communities. Cultivating and expanding that ongoing partnership will be critical to the long-term success of our operations. “As Kangwinan moves forward, and with the planned acquisition of the Aurukun Bauxite Project, we will keep listening and working together to create lasting value for Western Cape York, while respecting the connection Traditional Owners have to Country and making sure they continue to benefit sustainably from our operations. “This investment will support jobs, create opportunities for local and regional businesses, and help us continue supplying our customers with the high-quality bauxite they have relied on for decades.” Kangwinan, where early works began in 2025, will establish a new bauxite production centre 15 kilometres south-east of Rio Tinto’s Amrun mine, which is located 40km south of the town of Weipa. A 15-kilometre conveyor will transport ore from Kangwinan to expanded stockyard facilities and the existing Amrun wharf. The wharf will be expanded to accommodate larger capesize vessels, improving supply-chain efficiency and reducing freight costs. EXHIBIT 99.12

Notice to ASX/LSE Construction will ramp up in 2027 and is expected to support more than 1,300 construction jobs. Longer term, the project will help sustain Rio Tinto’s residentially based workforce of around 1,500 people in the Western Cape. In addition to its Kangwinan investment, Rio Tinto last month agreed to acquire, subject to government approvals, the adjacent Aurukun Bauxite Project from current owners Glencore (70%) and Mitsubishi (30%) to support its long-term growth plans in Western Cape York, using existing and planned infrastructure. The combined Amrun, Kangwinan and Aurukun operations are expected to support Weipa Operations well beyond the middle of this century. 1 All currency figures are in US dollars. Kangwinan’s total capital estimate of \$1.9 billion is inclusive of \$150 million for early works, which was pre-approved in 2025. This replacement capital expenditure is incorporated within Rio Tinto’s mid-term capital guidance. The investment is expected to deliver an internal rate of return of approximately 22%, based on third party market forecasts as at Q4 2026. Additional information Rio Tinto’s Australian aluminium operations span bauxite mines, alumina refineries and aluminium smelters, directly employing more than 5,000 people and supporting thousands more jobs across the country. The Kangwinan project, alongside recent agreements to support the future of the Bell Bay, Tomago and Boyne smelters, demonstrates how Rio Tinto is investing across its integrated aluminium value chain to support Australian production and jobs for years to come. The project was named Kangwinan at the request of the Wik Waya Traditional Owners. Kangwinan is a protector of all sacred, ceremonial and female protocols of the Wik Waya tribe. It is also the name of the black female crow, a totem of the Wik Waya people. Rio Tinto will continue to work with Traditional Owners to minimise impacts on cultural heritage, waterways and important access routes. Environmental permits for the Kangwinan project were previously secured through the State and Commonwealth-approved South of Embley Environmental Impact Statement (EIS). If the Aurukun acquisition proceeds, Rio Tinto would assess the appropriate environmental approvals pathway for its future development, in consultation with State and Commonwealth agencies, and work closely with Traditional Owners throughout the next stages of planning and development. ## Exhibit 99.13

EXHIBIT 99.13

Media Release

**Rio Tinto celebrates opening of \$1.4 billion lithium projects in Argentina**

29 October 2026

CATAMARCA, Argentina--(BUSINESS WIRE)-- President of Argentina Javier Milei and Catamarca Governor Raúl Jalil today joined Rio Tinto Chief Executive Simon Trott to celebrate a major milestone for Argentina’s lithium industry, marking the official opening of Rio Tinto’s Fénix 1B expansion and Sal de Vida operation in Catamarca following a combined investment of around \$1.4 billion.

Both projects achieved first production in the second quarter ahead of plan and are now ramping up production. They will add 25,000 tonnes of annual lithium carbonate equivalent (LCE) capacity once fully ramped up, strengthening Rio Tinto’s standing and Argentina’s position as a leading supplier of lithium for global markets.

The celebrations included a visit to Fénix by President Milei and Governor Jalil, hosted by Simon Trott, and an inauguration event recognising both projects with national and provincial authorities, employees, suppliers and representatives of local and Indigenous communities.

President of Argentina Javier Milei said: “The expansion of the Fénix project is a historic milestone that demonstrates the potential of Argentina’s mining sector. This was made possible by the country once again creating incentives for the production of resources that are essential to the modern world.

“We are proud that a company of this scale once again sees Argentina as a reliable partner and is investing in our country, validating the decisions we have taken over the past few years.”

Rio Tinto Chief Executive Simon Trott said: “Today is a proud moment for Rio Tinto and an important milestone for Argentina’s lithium industry, as we grow our position as a leading global supplier. It was a privilege to welcome President Milei, Governor Jalil and national and provincial authorities, and I thank them for joining us to recognise the work behind Fénix 1B and Sal de Vida.

“These two projects build on nearly three decades of lithium production in Catamarca and the expertise of our Argentine workforce. They demonstrate our industry-leading project delivery capability, as we execute with discipline towards our global target of 200,000 tonnes lithium carbonate equivalent annual production capacity by 2028.

“The delivery of these projects reflects the dedication of our people, the capabilities of Argentine suppliers and our partnerships with local communities and governments. Our focus now is on safely ramping up production, delivering reliable, high-quality lithium to our customers, and creating lasting value for our shareholders and Argentina.”

Fénix began operations in 1997 as Argentina’s first commercial lithium operation. The Fénix 1B expansion adds 10,000 tonnes of annual LCE capacity to the existing operation, increasing its total target of annual LCE capacity to approximately 40,000 tonnes.

Media Release

Sal de Vida is a new lithium carbonate operation located approximately 20 kilometres northeast of Fénix, with a target of annual LCE capacity of 15,000 tonnes.

The projects form part of Rio Tinto’s growing lithium business in Argentina, which includes operations and projects across Catamarca, Jujuy and Salta, including the Rincon lithium project.

Rio Tinto’s growth in Argentina is strengthening the country’s supplier network. Around 70% of the approximately \$4 billion in growth capital to be invested across our lithium operations in Argentina is expected to be spent with local suppliers.
